#### B28 strategic defense preparation (2026-10-29)

The live Strategist now prepares physical defenses when it has a fresh rival settlement within one additional reach of raid range and either directly believes MET-04/MET-05 or has contacted a rival publicly in Era 1 or later. It prepares at most two sites per civilization, ranks each rival's exposed sites by distance and then weaker existing defenses, queues a Palisade before its wood cost is fully stocked, and routes otherwise-unassigned material labor toward that wood. Under this direct signal, an unfinished non-defense project with at most 0.25 work-season can be redirected; its accumulated generic build progress carries to the Palisade. The focused B28/Strategist tests passed 65/65 in 5.4s, and the Release harness build passed with zero warnings/errors. A one-seed, two-mirror 64-minute screen took 22s and recorded 0/0 qualifying believed-Bronze attacks (5 settlement raids, 2 actual-Bronze launches), so it is diagnostic-only and does not establish efficacy. A public-era shadow still showed a 19.5 L Palisade wood gap at season 238 in one mirror; the 80% B28 threshold is unchanged. Output: `/tmp/civsim_b28_preparation_e2_20261019.log`.

**B28 Palisade wood-work trace (2026-10-29):** Corrected the proactive Palisade shortfall estimate from 8 L to the traced effective Chop yield of 1.8 L per work-season; ordinary metal-plan wood allocation retains its prior estimate and the material-work cap remains. Added bounded report-only tracing for the first five live prepared settlements. The one-seed, two-mirror, 64-minute replay completed in 22.9s, with 0/0 qualifying believed-Bronze attacks (8 settlement raids, 1 actual-Bronze launch, nonqualifying). At target s21, wood work was available early (0.70 at season 184), but material labor contracted to 0.04–0.06 by seasons 192–200 and was zero in seasons 208–232 while food consumed the available work; it reached 0.00 again at season 240. At season 248, s21 had 12/20 wood and 10.44 build progress. This identifies food-limited work capacity as the observed bottleneck in this replay; it does not show that the yield correction improved B28 outcomes. Focused B28/Strategist tests passed (11/11 for the added trace coverage; the earlier focused set passed 65/65), and the Release harness build succeeded with zero warnings/errors. The gate remains open and the 80% threshold is unchanged. Output: `/tmp/civsim_b28_preparation_e2_livework_20261021.log`.

**B28 pre-unlock preparation (2026-10-29):** Public Era 1 after contact now qualifies for preparation. The live policy can queue a Palisade before DEF-03 unlocks, stage wood with spare material work, and bank at most one Palisade's labor requirement; construction completion remains gated on DEF-03. The same one-seed, two-mirror, 64-minute replay completed in 21.9s. Live preparation began at season 35 (previously season 181 in the seed-20261021 trajectory); a prepared settlement had 21.9 L wood by season 50, and s0 completed its Palisade by season 168 after the technology became available. The progress cap prevented the pre-unlock queue from accumulating excess build labor. The match changed trajectory and still recorded 0/0 qualifying believed-Bronze attacks (8 settlement raids, 4 actual-Bronze launches), so this demonstrates earlier staging but not gate efficacy. Focused B28/Strategist tests passed 68/68; the Release harness build had zero warnings/errors. B28 remains open at the unchanged 80% threshold. Output: `/tmp/civsim_b28_era1_queued_palisade_capped_seed20261021.log`.

**B28 seed-20261019 earlier-preparation screen (2026-10-29):** The same bounded one-seed, two-mirror, 64-minute diagnostic completed in 23.8s with 0/0 qualifying believed-Bronze attacks (10 settlement raids, 5 actual-Bronze launches). The live policy accepted 16 preparation orders, earliest at season 46. One nonqualifying actual-Bronze attack hit s5, which had a Palisade in its pre-launch defense snapshot. This is a useful readiness example, but the defender did not directly believe Bronze, so it is not a qualifying B28 case. The B28 gate remains open at 80%. #### Runtime profile and forecast-rollout reduction (2026-10-30)

A bounded 15-second EventPipe profile found `ResearchSpark.Search` at 11.7% inclusive time and `ResearchForecast.Run` at 9.23%; the largest reported exclusive methods were `GenerateKnowHow` (2.93%) and `Step` (2.12%). Thread waits accounted for 63.6% of samples, so this is a partial CPU profile rather than a complete attribution. The profile and source review identified an avoidable 120-season B29 report-only rollout on every Spark decision after KEY-3, even though B29 consumes only the first post-KEY-3 estimate. The rollout is now disabled in ordinary matches, enabled by the B29 diagnostic, and cached after its first run. Forecast rollouts also no longer allocate a `ForecastBronzeRollout` object each time, and `DoneAt` cleanup clears only the entries completed by the previous rollout. Focused B29/Spark tests passed 20/20 (11s); the earlier forecast-focused suite passed 12/12. The one-seed, two-mirror, 64-minute B28 screen for seed 20261020 took 30.46s including the `dotnet run` invocation and 32.12s in a repeat without rebuilding, versus 37.9s in the prior same-seed run. Both post-change screens had 0/0 qualifying attacks and were diagnostic only. This is a promising 15–20% runtime reduction on a small sample. Outputs: `/tmp/civsim_b28_runtime_after_opt_seed20261020.log` and `/tmp/civsim_b28_runtime_after_opt_seed20261020_repeat.log`.

**B28 exact eight-seed cohort after runtime reduction (2026-10-30):** Ran the exact 64-minute seed range 20261019–20261026 in four sequential two-seed shards, each capped at 90s. Shard runtimes were 37.0s, 52.5s, 52.9s, and 54.7s (3m17s total). Aggregate telemetry across 16 matches was 0/1 defenses before a qualifying believed-Bronze raid (0%), with 30 settlement raids, six actual-Bronze launches, and one believed-Bronze opportunity. Applying the current 80% threshold to the combined qualifying sample, B28 fails; the denominator is only one opportunity, so this result is weak evidence about defense balance. Each shard individually reported diagnostic-only because it had fewer than eight seeds; the combined contiguous cohort meets the eight-seed and 64-minute gate floor. Outputs: `/tmp/civsim_b28_runtime_two_seed_shard_20261019.log`, `..._20261021.log`, `..._20261023.log`, and `..._20261025.log`.

**B28 high-confidence target coverage and Auto wood labor screen (2026-10-29; pre-cost-change):** Focused policy/build/Strategist tests passed 75/75 in 6s. Preparation now ranks directly believed Bronze and contacted public-E3 rivals ahead of generic Era-1/2 contacts, reserves two high-confidence settlement targets plus one generic hedge, covers separate high-confidence rivals before a second site, and allows urgent prep to redirect an active ordinary build while preserving progress. Auto scouts at an underfunded queued Palisade are released one at a time only when the reduced scout plan still forecasts full winter food coverage; explicit scout settings are unchanged. The bounded seed-20261022, two-mirror, 64-minute screen completed in about 37s with 0/0 qualifying attacks; its one actual Bronze raid hit s18 after a Palisade had completed, but the defender did not yet believe Bronze, so this is readiness-only evidence. A second bounded screen on seed-20261019 completed in about 37s with 0/1 qualifying attacks defended: the Warlord's s0 had been queued since season 85 but had only 11 L wood at season 206, 8.5 L at 208, and 1.3 L at 212; the 20 L Palisade remained unbuilt as hearth use consumed most seasonal chop output. This points to a hard wood/food-work constraint, and the Auto diversion did not establish gate efficacy. Do not run the long B28 cohort from these screens. B28 remains open at its unchanged 80% threshold. Outputs: `/tmp/civsim_b28_autoscout_wood_seed20261022.log` and `/tmp/civsim_b28_autoscout_wood_seed20261019.log`.

#### B28 additional opportunities and bounded tuning screens (2026-10-30)

Screened 24 further baseline seeds in sequential two-seed shards with a 90-second cap per shard (each completed in 23–55 seconds). Across seeds 20261019–20261050, the 32-seed baseline now has 5/11 defended qualifying cases (45%): the original 8 seeds produced 0/1, seeds 20261027–20261042 produced 3/6, and seeds 20261043–20261050 produced 2/4. This remains far below the unchanged 80% B28 gate. The denominator is still modest, but the traces now include repeated resource/lead-time failures as well as missed target coverage.

Tested and removed two narrow tuning candidates. Reducing only tier-0 Palisade cost from 8 L to 6 L produced 2/7 defended cases over the matched 16-seed block 20261027–20261042, versus baseline 3/6; the opportunity denominator changed, so this is not a clean paired rate comparison, and it provides no support for the extra cost reduction. Raising the high-confidence target cap from two to three left the first three traced shards unchanged at 0/1, 1/2, and 0/1. A direct-Bronze capital fallback, first only when no target existed and then using the spare hedge slot, also left seed 20261045–20261046 unchanged at 1/3. All candidates were removed; the current tier-0 cost remains 8 L, the Village total 16 L, and the high-confidence cap two plus one generic hedge.

Representative traces explain why these changes did not move the score. In seed 20261033, the attacked s3 Palisade had accumulated its required labor but held 7.4 L against an 8 L cost in season 199; it completed after the raid. In seed 20261046, the AI queued a Palisade for s32 one season before the season-242 attack. In seed 20261045, two first qualifying attacks hit the same defender's s2 across the mirror matches; one had a Palisade and one did not, while the missed case had no recorded B28 order trail. The data points to mixed failure modes, including late exposure, incomplete construction, and target selection; no single threshold or cap change is supported yet. Outputs: `/tmp/civsim_b28_opportunity_shard_20261027.log` through `..._20261041.log`, `/tmp/civsim_b28_more_baseline_shard_20261043.log` through `..._20261049.log`, `/tmp/civsim_b28_more_baseline_trace_20261045.log`, `/tmp/civsim_b28_cap3_trace_shard_20261029.log` through `..._20261033.log`, `/tmp/civsim_b28_capital_slot_trace_20261045.log`, and `/tmp/civsim_b28_palisade6_candidate_shard_20261027.log` through `..._20261041.log`.

#### A3 campaign and GOV-02 research screens (2026-10-30)

The exact A3 gate remains 6/16 Warlord wins with 16/16 clean survival. Several bounded screens did not support campaign-force or posture-unlock tuning. The default-off Levy staging candidate matched the no-candidate result on seeds 20261025–20261026 (2/4 wins, no Levy staged) and has been removed. Current-tree screens combining 2× rival-pace estimates with max-force origin selection stayed at 2/4 on both seed pairs 20261021–20261022 and 20261025–20261026; increasing the diagnostic estimate to 3.5× still left 20261021–20261022 at 2/4. A direct GOV-02 bonus override of 72 also remained at 2/4 on 20261021–20261022, with no Warlord raids or assaults.

A follow-up default-off candidate propagated that bonus to the currently available prerequisites in GOV-02's dependency chain. It fell to 0/4 Warlord wins on seeds 20261021–20261022, versus the current baseline's 2/4. The Warlord made no foundings, raids, declarations, or assaults in those matches; GOV-02 remained locked for 286–511 of 488–512 seasons. This indicates that prioritizing the chain displaced expansion without opening the posture path. The candidate was removed after a focused test (1/1, 5.96s) and Release harness build (zero warnings/errors, 0.49s). No production behavior or acceptance threshold changed. #### A3 early-food and scout-allocation screen (2026-10-30)

Report-only 16-minute traces on seeds 20261021–20261022 show a losing Warlord orientation entering a population/food-work decline before it can fission. In seed 20261021 mirror 1, Warlord s1 moved from 23 people and 4.78 food work at season 40 to 21 people and 2.83 food work at season 56 (10.1 food output against 19.0 need, with two starvation deaths), then 17 people and 1.75 food work at season 64. The matched mirror remained healthy and expanded. A repeated narrow emergency-food-work screen on seeds 20261021–20261022 scored 1/4 Warlord wins versus 2/4 baseline, confirming that this labor override does not rescue the pair.

A new default-off counterfactual capped Warlord Auto scouting at one scout whenever opening stores were below current seasonal need, preserving the normal policy otherwise. On the same two-seed, 64-minute pair it scored 0/4 wins versus baseline 2/4; clean survival remained 4/4, and no settlement losses occurred. The inconsistent settlement changes do not justify trading away scouting for this threshold, so the candidate wiring was removed. No production rule or acceptance threshold changed. A3 remains open at the exact current-source 6/16 win result and 16/16 clean survival. A final diagnostic used exact current-season food need and opening stores to let stock coverage raise food-research urgency before prior-season food security declined. It fell to 1/4 on the original pair versus 2/4 baseline (Legacy 3,858.3 vs 4,319.1). All screens had clean survival and no settlement losses; none supports a production change. The temporary urgency and bonus overrides were removed. The earlier exact-cohort campaign rejections motivated the follow-up attacker and rally trace below. **A3 attacker-availability and rally trace (2026-10-30):** The current-source, two-seed, 64-minute mirrored diagnostic stayed at 2/4 Warlord wins and 4/4 clean survival, with no Warlord assault launched. None of the selected Warlord campaign options was launch-feasible. In the seed-20261021 losing orientation, both Warlord seats had zero available attackers; stronger Warlord positions sometimes had 10–19 attackers but were blocked by force ratio or counter-threat exposure, including cases where leaving for the campaign would expose a settlement to a much larger rival army. This does not support lowering the six-attacker floor or home reserve: low-army cases reflect a missing population/fighter base, while the other cases fail later strategic safety checks. Shift the next A3 screen to early growth and fission timing; campaign pressure is downstream of that shortfall. Output: `/tmp/civsim-a3-attacker-timing-trace-seed20261021-22-64m.txt`.

**A3 GOV-02 research-priority trace (2026-10-30):** The same bounded four-match baseline showed research-path asymmetry around the Expand unlock. In the seed-20261021 losing orientation, the traced Warlord seat never put GOV-02 or SOC-06/SOC-07 in its plan, live queue, or top-six research candidates across 311 E2 decisions (seasons 93–256). In the winning orientation, GOV-02 first appeared in the plan at season 124. Seed 20261022's losing orientation did plan and queue GOV-02, but still remained GOV-02-locked for 429 of 492 seasons. The earlier large direct bonus and prerequisite-chain bonus screens did not improve wins, so this trace does not support another bonus override. Together with the zero-attacker collapse in seed 20261021, this points the next bounded A3 investigation toward early growth/fission readiness and the conditions under which Warlord can sustain research, rather than another war-launch threshold. Output: `/tmp/civsim-a3-gov02-priority-trace-seed20261021-22-64m.txt`.

**A3 fission and food-buffer screens (2026-10-31):** A 16-minute, four-match trace across seeds 20261021–20261022 confirmed that the seed-20261021 losing Warlord orientation collapses before fission: one settlement fell from 23 people/eight adults at season 40 to 17 people/three adults at season 64, with 13 deaths (five starvation, three injury, two endemic, two predator, one violence) against 25 births; its fission-pressure flag stayed false. The only Warlord fission in that baseline sample occurred in seed 20261022 mirror 0 at season 59, and its daughter already received three adult women, so a deterministic second-woman cohort reservation made no selection or outcome change and was removed. The losing Warlord's research trace did not queue FOD-03 until season 64. A separate counterfactual that gave each Warlord starting settlement a Storage Pit from season 0 held at 2/4 wins on the same short pair; in the seed-20261021 losing mirror, the weakest Warlord settlement ended at 14 people with six starvation deaths, versus 17 people and five starvation deaths at baseline. The early buffer grant therefore did not rescue the diagnosed collapse, and no production rule or acceptance threshold changed. This remains diagnostic-only evidence; A3's exact acceptance result is still 6/16 wins and 16/16 clean survival. Outputs: `/tmp/civsim-a3-growth-collapse-trace-seed20261021-22-16m.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-second-woman-baseline-seed20261021-22-16m.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-second-woman-candidate-seed20261021-22-16m.txt`, and `/tmp/civsim-a3-early-storage-pit-seed20261021-22-16m.txt`.

**A3 winter scout-decision trace and screen (2026-10-31):** A report-only death/decision trace on the same short pair found a fully healthy age-41 adult Warlord scout die at season 55; winter-shortfall hazard was 0.024 of 0.028 total. That decision row had 3.38 food against 19.03 need, prior food security 1.00, and two scouts selected; the winter forecast was 0.883 with the selected plan and 1.000 with none. A default-off diagnostic released Auto scouts on winter turns until the forecast reached full coverage. The original two-seed pair remained 2/4 Warlord wins in both arms with identical mean Warlord Legacy (1,101.25); the held-out 20261023–20261024 pair also remained 2/4, with mean Warlord Legacy slightly lower (1,142.0→1,137.8). Although the diagnosed mirror's season-56 attribution row had no deaths under the candidate versus two at baseline, the effect did not repeat as an A3 win improvement; remove the behavior-changing override. Keep only report-only scout-decision telemetry for future diagnosis. No production rule or gate changed; the exact A3 result remains 6/16 wins and 16/16 clean survival. Outputs: `/tmp/civsim-a3-scout-decision-trace-seed20261021-22-16m.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-winter-scout-release-candidate-seed20261021-22-16m.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-winter-scout-release-baseline-seed20261023-24-16m.txt`, and `/tmp/civsim-a3-winter-scout-release-candidate-seed20261023-24-16m.txt`.

**A3 pre-E3 fission-threshold scope screens (2026-10-31):** Exact eight-seed, 64-minute screens show that threshold scaling is not a robust win-rate fix. Applying a diagnostic 0.60 scale to all pre-E3 Warlord settlements raised wins from 6/16 to 8/16, but mean Warlord Legacy fell slightly (4,845.7→4,816.2), mean Warlord Firsts Legacy fell 714.2→471.8, and the gate still failed. Scales of 0.65 and 0.70 each scored 4/16. Restricting 0.60 to original settlements or, separately, to first-generation daughters both returned to 6/16 (mean Warlord Legacy 4,632.4 and 4,987.0 respectively). Clean survival remained 16/16 in all three scoped/all-settlement runs; none lost a Warlord settlement. The broad 0.60 arm increased below-six campaign rejections from 182 to 318, so its two-win lift does not establish improved military readiness. Short two-seed screens had looked favorable for the original-only scope, but the exact cohort reversed that signal. No production threshold changed. Stop fission-threshold tuning and return to the pre-fission decline: compare the failing Warlord seat's age/health cohort and food-work capacity against its healthy mirror to find the first demographic divergence. Outputs: `/tmp/civsim-a3-pre-e3-fission-060-candidate-exact8x64.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-pre-e3-fission-065-candidate-exact8x64.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-pre-e3-fission-070-candidate-exact8x64.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-pre-e3-original-only-060-exact8x64.txt`, and `/tmp/civsim-a3-pre-e3-first-daughter-060-exact8x64.txt`.

**A3 winter-scout recheck and exact screen (2026-10-31):** The baseline season-55 scout-decision row showed 0.668 projected winter coverage with two scouts versus 1.000 with none, but the policy kept both because its winter-forecast check only runs in the preceding seasonal slot. A diagnostic correction re-evaluated coverage during winter and released Auto scouts until the forecast reached one. The short 20261021–20261022 pair stayed at 2/4 Warlord wins; the traced season-55 decision selected one scout and reported final forecast 1.000, but the age-41 Warlord scout still died by season 64. The exact 8×64 cohort then fell to 3/16 wins and mean Legacy 4,585.2 versus 6/16 and 4,845.7 under the source baseline. This broader release harms the A3 gate, so the candidate was removed. The current scout policy and all acceptance thresholds remain unchanged; A3 remains open at 6/16 wins and 16/16 clean survival. Outputs: `/tmp/civsim-a3-winter-scout-coverage-fix-seed20261021-22-16m.txt` and `/tmp/civsim-a3-winter-scout-coverage-fix-exact8x64.txt`.

**A3 incoming-raid threat screen and exact cohort (2026-10-31):** A focused Strategist regression confirmed that a visible incoming raid is recorded in belief with attacker, target, arrival, and party size, but `ReadThreat` did not consume that evidence. A candidate used the observed party size against its actual target settlement and set attack probability to one while the raid was en route. The focused Release test passed (1/1, 1s), and a two-seed, 16-minute screen completed in 7.28s at 2/4 Warlord wins, unchanged from the small-pair baseline. The exact eight-seed, 64-minute cohort completed in 72.10s at 5/16 wins and 16/16 clean survival, versus the source baseline 6/16 and 16/16; mean Legacy was 4,657.5 vs. 5,907.2 for Warlord/Rusher, also worse than the corresponding baseline. Since the candidate did not improve the gate and lowered Warlord wins, the behavior and regression were removed. No A3 threshold changed. The next investigation returns to the pre-fission demographic decline. Outputs: `/tmp/civsim-a3-incoming-raid-screen-seed20261021-22-16m.txt` and `/tmp/civsim-a3-incoming-raid-exact8x64.txt`.

**A3 fertility attribution and seat-completeness check (2026-10-31):** Added default-off report-only telemetry at the actual conception step: fertile female age bands, pregnancy/illness/wound counts, surviving conception attempts, conceptions and completed births, fertile-woman deaths, prior-season food security, fertility-health and crowding factors, and the caps/chances used by the model. The telemetry test passed 1/1 (5s) with identical checksums and Legacy when tracing is enabled; Release build had zero warnings/errors. Two bounded 16-minute pairs finished in 8.67s and 8.38s. Their rows show that the seed-20261021 mirror1 collapse is local, not a general Warlord fertility penalty: the same mirror0 Warlord seats s0 and s3 had full prior food security, four deaths each, and s0 had more fertile women/attempts than s3; in the collapsing mirror1, s1 fell below s2 in fertile women, attempts, and conceptions, while s2 had more starvation deaths. Across held-out seeds 20261023–20261024, Warlords averaged 0.32 fewer fertile women and 0.22 fewer attempts than Rusher seats in 396 matched s1–s3 season rows, but prior food security was nearly tied and health/per-woman chance differences changed sign. The evidence points to localized adult-cohort and later food collapse, with stochastic conception outcomes, and does not justify a production fertility or mortality adjustment. Settlement 0 is now included in the trace. No gate or game behavior changed. Outputs: `/tmp/civsim-a3-fertility-trace-seed20261021-22-16m.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-fertility-trace-seed20261023-24-16m.txt`, and `/tmp/civsim-a3-fertility-trace-all-seats-seed20261021-22-16m.txt`.

**A3 sex-resolved death trace (2026-10-31):** Added sex and pregnancy status to the opt-in death-state trace and extended the report-only telemetry test to check both fields. The focused test passed 1/1 (8s; 15s command wall time), and a two-seed, 16-minute trace finished in 8s. In seed 20261021 mirror1's collapsing Warlord s1, two nonpregnant women aged 29 and 27 died before the first food shortfall (violence at season 30; endemic disease at season 39); s1 later lost children and adults to winter-shortfall starvation. Its counterpart s2 also had child-heavy winter-starvation deaths, but its pregnant 40-year-old adult death at season 56 was endemic disease with no starvation hazard. Across the other sampled Warlord seats, pre-shortfall adult-woman deaths were absent or isolated (injury/exposure), so this is a localized stochastic cohort loss, not evidence of a repeatable sex-specific mortality rule. No production balance change is supported; A3 remains at its previously measured 6/16 wins and 16/16 clean survival. Output: `/tmp/civsim-a3-sex-death-trace-seed20261021-22-16m.txt`.

**A3 forage/hunt return screen (2026-10-31):** At the first divergence window in seed 20261021 mirror1, Warlord s1 produced 11.56 food against 18.03 need and s2 produced 17.03 against 19.94, while growing Rusher s3 produced 32.53 against 23.32. The short seed-20261021–20261024 traces had 586 food-short Warlord rows with both jobs staffed: realized yield averaged 4.79 food/forage work versus 1.07/hunt work, with forage at least 1.5× the hunt return in 573/586 rows. The same pattern appeared for Rushers (5.09 versus 1.26; 539/554 rows). A default-off diagnostic reduced the Warlord hunt-work cap from 30% to 10% during a food shortage only when the prior season's forage return was at least 1.5× hunt return. In the 20261021–20261022 16-minute pair it held at 2/4 wins (baseline 2/4), with Legacy moving from 1,099.7 to 1,117.3. On the held-out 20261023–20261024 pair it regressed from 3/4 to 2/4 wins and Warlord Legacy from 1,141.2 to 1,090.8. High realized forage return therefore did not predict that diverting more work would help; the candidate and its harness wiring were removed without running the long cohort. The production 70/30 food-work mix is unchanged. A3 remains open at its last exact 6/16 wins and 16/16 clean survival. Outputs: `/tmp/civsim-a3-foragefirst-baseline-seed20261021-22-16m.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-foragefirst-candidate-v2-seed20261021-22-16m.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-foragefirst-baseline-seed20261023-24-16m.txt`, and `/tmp/civsim-a3-foragefirst-candidate-seed20261023-24-16m.txt`.

**A3 forage-stock versus work-capacity check (2026-10-31):** Added report-only forage potential and accessible stock immediately before harvest. The focused demography/food-attribution test passed 1/1 in 10 seconds with tracing still checksum- and Legacy-neutral. A two-seed, 16-minute trace took under 12 seconds. At season 44 in seed 20261021 mirror1, forage harvest equaled its full work-based potential for all three seats: Warlord s1 had 10.77 potential / 37.38 accessible stock, s2 15.38 / 23.82, and Rusher s3 29.53 / 35.93. Forage stock was not binding. The immediate gap was effective adult work available to food: 5.50 for s1 and 6.75 for s2 versus 8.00 for s3; the Warlord settlements also had fewer adults (7/8 versus 9). This supports the cohort/work-capacity diagnosis, not another forage/hunt mix change. No production behavior or A3 acceptance result changed. Output: `/tmp/civsim-a3-forage-stock-trace-seed20261021-22-16m.txt`.

**A3 gated pre-E3 fission scale diagnostics (2026-10-31):** Two separate default-off candidates applied the 0.60 Warlord pre-E3 cap-pressure scale conditionally while leaving late-winter pressure unchanged: the parent-floor variant required at least 12 projected people to remain in the parent after a 40% split; the food-security variant required current parent FoodSecurity ≥0.80. On the three short pairs (seeds 20261021–20261022, 20261023–20261024, and 20261025–20261026; both mirrors; 16 minutes each), the food-security candidate scored 11/12 Warlord wins versus 8/12 for baseline. This directional short-run lift did not reproduce in either exact cohort. The parent-floor exact 8-seed, 64-minute cohort completed in 118.7 seconds and scored 6/16 wins, 16/16 clean survival, mean Warlord Legacy 5,083.5 versus the 4,845.7 baseline; below-six campaign rejections still short were 308 (34 rescued). It flipped four outcomes versus baseline (two in each direction). The food-security exact cohort completed in 57.07 seconds and also scored 6/16 wins and 16/16 clean survival, with mean Warlord Legacy 4,777.9 versus 4,845.7 baseline; below-six campaign rejections still short were 299 (23 rescued). It flipped six outcomes, three in each direction. Both temporary harness/simulation wirings and their focused test were removed; no production threshold changed. A third default-off combined-safety candidate applied 0.60 only when both current food security was ≥0.80 and projected post-split parent population was ≥12; late-winter pressure stayed unchanged. Its 20261021–20261022 16-minute pair scored 4/4 Warlord wins and mean Legacy 1,195.0 vs 963.5 for Rusher, versus the same-pair baseline 2/4 and 1,099.7 vs 1,067.0. On held-out seeds 20261025–20261026 it scored 3/4. The fission trace still included two Warlord daughter-side negative projected four-season margins (−0.48 and −10.50), so short-run scores did not establish fission safety. The combined candidate's exact 8-seed, 64-minute cohort completed in 37.93 seconds and scored 6/16 Warlord wins (same as baseline), 16/16 clean survival, mean Legacy 5,097.7 Warlord / 5,482.1 Rusher versus baseline Warlord 4,845.7; below-six campaign rejections still short were 306 (34 rescued). It flipped four outcomes, two in each direction. Thus the three-pair food-security short-screen lift of 11/12 vs 8/12 did not survive the exact win metric. All three temporary harness/simulation wirings and focused tests were removed; no production threshold changed. Outputs: `/tmp/civsim-a3-fission-parent-floor12-seed20261021-22-16m.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-fission-parent-floor12-seed20261023-24-16m.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-fission-parent-floor12-exact8x64.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-fission-foodsecurity-gate-seed20261021-22-16m.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-fission-foodsecurity-gate-seed20261025-26-16m.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-fission-foodsecurity-gate-exact8x64.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-fission-combined-safety-seed20261021-22-16m.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-fission-combined-safety-seed20261025-26-16m.txt`, and `/tmp/civsim-a3-fission-combined-safety-exact8x64.txt`.

**A3 held-out adult-cohort trace and loss classification (2026-10-31):** The report-only 16-minute trace on held-out seeds 20261023–20261024 completed in about six seconds. The early worker-capacity gap is seat-local: in seed 20261024 mirror 0, Warlord s0 had seven adults and 5.50 effective capacity at season 40, then fell to seven people (two aged 14–19) by season 64; Warlord s3 in the same match grew from nine adults and 9.00 capacity to 30 people by season 64. In the other mirror, Warlord s1 grew while s2 stagnated; seed 20261023 mirror 0 also had a collapsing Rusher seat. This does not support a general Warlord fertility, mortality, or work-capacity rule. Separately, parsing the exact current-source 8×64 baseline by outcome shows the A3 win losses are expansion-heavy: in Warlord wins it averaged 14.8 settlements and 12.2 splits versus Rusher's 11.5 and 10.5; in Warlord losses it averaged 10.5 settlements and 8.4 splits versus Rusher's 21.3 and 19.4. The baseline remains 6/16 wins and 16/16 clean survival. The broad pre-E3 0.60 fission threshold confirms that expansion timing can flip outcomes, but it also flipped two Warlord wins into losses while rescuing four; its final cohort result was only 8/16. Keep the production threshold unchanged. The next A3 intervention should target the conditions for *safe* early expansion/fission, with a short paired screen first; further food-work, scout, sex-specific demographic, or unconditional fission-threshold tuning is not supported. Output: `/tmp/civsim-a3-adult-cohort-trace-seed20261023-24-16m.txt` (the exact baseline and threshold-screen outputs are cited in the entries above).

**A3 Found-funnel and frontier-site follow-up (2026-10-31):** Extended the opt-in Found funnel with allowed-EV and forecast-refusal summaries, and added a 16-season report-only frontier-site trajectory. On seeds 20261021–20261024 at 64 minutes, Warlord won 2/8 matches; wins had 18–20 Warlord settlements, while the held-out 20261023–20261024 pair stayed 0/4 with Warlord behind on settlements in all four matches. In seed 20261023 mirror 0, Warlord civ0 had 49 seasons with raw Found candidates but no allowed Found forecast; the refusal tally was 7,198 NoSite and 4,144 OutOfReach tile evaluations (counts are forecast evaluations, not seasons). The 20261023 trajectory shows site availability diverging by seat at season 32, before Mandate unlock: one Warlord seat had zero eligible sites while its paired Rusher had 222, while the other Warlord seat was near parity. Rusher first unlocked Mandate at season 176; Warlord civ0 unlocked at 192 and briefly had the Found gate open with 17–24 projected frontier sites at seasons 208–240, but no Found decree followed. A Warlord-only +6 land FoundReach counterfactual reduced OutOfReach forecasts but did not make that seat's forecasts legal or add a Found; on seeds 20261023–20261024 it stayed 0/4 wins and 4/4 clean survival, with 2 total Warlord Found decrees in both arms and mean Warlord settlements 9.5→9.0. The override was removed; no production behavior or A3 gate changed. The baseline remains 6/16 wins and 16/16 clean survival. Reconcile the open-gate/projected-site checkpoints with the zero-allowed-funnel result before considering another expansion rule. Outputs: `/tmp/civsim-a3-found-funnel-seed20261021-24-64m.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-found-refusal-smoke-seed20261023-64m.txt`, `/tmp/civsim-a3-found-frontier-trajectory-seed20261023-64m.txt`, and `/tmp/civsim-a3-found-reach-land-plus6-seed20261023-24-64m.txt`.

The paired control and candidate both won 4/16 matches (25%) with no winner flips and full survival. **A3 current-source pre-E3 fission-scale rerun (2026-10-31):** Re-ran the strongest prior broad fission candidate, `--warlord-pre-e3-fission-threshold-scale-override 0.60`, against the paired current-source no-flag control on seeds 20261021–20261028, both mirrors, at 64 minutes (16 matches per arm; candidate completed in about 32 seconds). Candidate scored 5/16 Warlord wins versus 4/16 control, with four winner flips: two Rusher→Warlord and two Warlord→Rusher. The candidate raised total Warlord settlements/splits 195/166→223/191 while Found decrees fell 20→6; Warlord lost no settlements in either arm, and captures fell 4→1. **A3 pre-E3 fission scale point check (2026-10-31):** The all-Warlord pre-E3 0.55 threshold override ran on the exact same eight seeds, both mirrors, and 64-minute horizon in about 38 seconds. It scored 3/16 wins, below both the no-flag control (4/16) and 0.60-only (5/16). Against control it flipped five outcomes (two Rusher→Warlord, three Warlord→Rusher); versus 0.60 it flipped two, both Warlord wins to Rusher. This lower scale expands further but degrades wins, captures and Firsts. **A3 balanced fission cohort screen (2026-10-31):** A default-off Warlord pre-E3 selection candidate attempted to retain at least three 14–44-year-old adults in both parent and daughter, while preserving the existing mandatory daughter members and falling back to the exact prior sample when infeasible. Its focused sampler/invariance tests passed 7/7, and Release built cleanly. On seeds 20261021–20261024, both mirrors, 16 minutes, the candidate and matched control were identical in all eight outcomes (5/8 Warlord wins), Legacy (W/R 1120.5/1052.4; D/M/F/W 811/191/118.5/0 and 789/187.2/76.1/0), settlement losses (0 for either side), Warlord/Rusher settlements (20/21), and splits (4/5); all 392 per-season settlement-trace rows were byte-identical. **A3 campaign-plan shadow diagnostic (2026-10-31):** Extended the existing opt-in `--warlord-campaign-opportunity-trace` with one report-only row after `ChoosePlan`, joining the already-evaluated campaign options for that season to the selected plan and grouping option blockers. It does not re-evaluate options, issue orders, or affect selection. Release build succeeded with 0 warnings/errors; the existing focused checksum/seat-isolation test was extended and passed (1/1, 9s). The current-source seeds 20261021–20261022, both mirrors, 64-minute screen completed in 29.2s under the 60s cap: Warlord won 2/4 and both sides had 0 settlement losses (4/4 clean survival), matching the short-pair baseline. In 1,002 Warlord plan snapshots, selected plans were Defend 965 and FrontierRace 37; none was Conquer. Across evaluated campaign options, there were **zero** positive, launch-feasible campaigns while a non-Conquer plan was selected (and zero feasible campaigns overall). The blocker tally was dominated by no-known-target 637, force-ratio 439, attacker-count 184, and capture-chance 112 (option rows can count multiple targets per decision; categories are counts, not disjoint seasons). Thus the AI-plan-bottleneck hypothesis is falsified for this sample: it did not decline an available safe campaign; the cases fail before plan choice, consistent with an army/target/evaluation bottleneck. Keep this as diagnostic evidence only; it does not justify a plan-priority or balance change. Output: `/tmp/civsim-a3-plan-shadow-20261021-22-64m.txt`.

**A3 combat-tech readiness shadow (2026-10-31):** A report-only weapon-tech projection was screened on the current-source seeds 20261021–20261022, both mirrors, at 64 minutes; all four matches completed in about 41 seconds under the 60-second cap. Warlord won 2/4 and both sides had zero settlement losses (4/4 clean survival). The trace contained 278 campaign options with a measurable force upper bound; 145 were blocked by the tactical force-ratio gate. None of those 145 had an immediately visible, incomplete weapon-upgrade step available to project, so **zero force-ratio blockers crossed the 1.5 gate** under a legal next-tech shadow. Four other trace rows had HNT-06 visible as the next step, but they were not force-ratio-blocked options. The diagnostic and extra research-state plumbing were removed because the candidate had no actionable tech crossover in the observed blocker set. No research priority, combat behavior, or acceptance gate changed. Output: `/private/tmp/civsim-a3-combat-tech-shadow-20261021-22-64m.txt`.

**A3 Warlord food-capacity screen (2026-10-31):** A Warlord-seat-only diagnostic raised usable food capacity from ×1.00 to ×1.10 through the `FoodValue` multiplier while leaving fertility, labor assignment, fission, scouts, campaigns, and Rusher rules unchanged. On seeds 20261021–20261024, both mirrors, 16 minutes, it scored 4/8 Warlord wins plus one tie versus 5/8 wins for control; mean Warlord Legacy fell 1120.5→1100.8 and owned land share fell 1.8%→1.4%. Neither arm lost a settlement. Food-security mean was unchanged at 0.95 and starvation remained 0.07 deaths per traced Warlord settlement-season; the screen showed no fission or growth improvement that offset the lower win/Legacy results. Remove the override and do not run held-out or exact cohorts. An initial paired run was discarded because the override was wired to the `EconomyArchetype.Warlord` profile while the duel seats use `Balanced`; only the corrected per-civilization run above is evidence. No production food balance or A3 gate changed. **A3 early demographic trace for the severe-loss seeds (2026-10-31):** A report-only 16-minute trace on seeds 20261021–20261024 (both mirrors; 12.7 seconds) compared the early path for the four official mirror1 losses that ended with 2–6 Warlord settlements. At season 16, their Warlord starting settlements still had 13–20 people and full sampled food security, so a visible food deficit was not present at the start. Warlord starvation deaths nevertheless appeared by seasons 7–19; the first seasonal food-ledger shortages followed at seasons 24–37. Examples include seed 20261021 s2 at season 37 (7.1 opening food, 12.9 pre-eat food vs 16.4 need, 9 adults / 9.12 capacity) and seed 20261024 s2 at season 24 (0.5 opening food, 7.3 vs 12.9, 5 adults / 4.29 capacity). Warlord did not fission by season 64 in seeds 20261021/22; in seed 20261023 one fission followed a shortfall by one season, while seed 20261024 fissioned with 3.8 pre-stores. These are observed trajectories, not counterfactual proof: food pressure and starvation accompany the severe losses but also occur in some non-collapse mirrors, and this 16-minute trace ends before most of the score divergence. No rule change is supported by this sample. Output: `/private/tmp/civsim-a3-collapse-current-seed20261021-24-16m.txt`.

A 64-minute follow-up on seeds 20261021–20261022 (both mirrors; 23.7 seconds) shows two different loss paths. In seed 20261021 mirror1, Warlord population fell from 34 across two settlements at season 64 to 23 at season 80 and 11 at season 128; starvation accounted for 48 of its 108 recorded deaths, and no Warlord fission appeared through season128. Rusher grew from 65 people/three settlements to 116/four. Warlord's first food shortfall (season37, s2) preceded the first recorded Rusher raid (season43); seven successful incoming raids later hit s1 during seasons43–71 as its food and population declined. Raids may have amplified the collapse, but the trace does not isolate their causal effect. In seed 20261022 mirror1, Warlord had 47 people/two settlements vs Rusher's 60/three at season64; Warlord fissioned at season80, but by season128 trailed 53/three to 132/five. It finished at 3,324 vs 7,165 Legacy and 3.9% vs 20.0% land, again without losing a settlement. The traces suggest both a food-starvation collapse path and a separate expansion-rate path; no single raid or fission threshold change is supported. Settlement snapshots stop at season128 even though death-state rows continue to terminal seasons 247/255. Output: `/private/tmp/civsim-a3-collapse-current-seed20261021-22-64m.txt`.

**A3 extended food/work attribution trace (2026-10-31):** Extended the opt-in `--warlord-settlement-trace` food ledger and work attribution from seasons 24–80 through 128, and removed the prior settlement-ID-4 ceiling so newly founded settlements are sampled too. This is report-only. Release build passed with 0 warnings/errors; the focused `Settlement_trace_demography_and_extended_food_work_window_are_report_only` test passed (1/1, 10s), verifying post-season-80 ledger and work rows plus identical checksum and Legacy with tracing enabled. The requested seeds 20261021–20261022, both mirrors, 64-minute run completed in about 16s under the 90s cap. In the official seed20261021 mirror1 loss, Warlord s1 went from 5 adults / 3.18 weighted adult capacity / 1.99 assigned food work at season81 to 1 / 0.65 / 0.38 at season128; its actual forage+meat yield fell from 8.19 to 1.25 while need fell 8.0 to 5.8 and stores stayed at zero. Across seasons81–128 it had 10 ledger shortfalls. S2 fell from 4 adults / 4.25 weighted capacity / 2.70 food work to zero adults / zero capacity / 0.13 food work; actual yield fell 7.57 to 0.45, need 9.6 to 1.2, stores stayed at zero, and 39/48 ledger rows were short. Rusher continued raiding s1: 12 successful resolutions in this window with reported loot totaling 176, plus a season125 raid still unresolved by season128. S2 was not the target and also collapsed. This supports a demographic/work-capacity feedback loop alongside the raid pressure, but cannot quantify causal contribution of raid loot.

In seed20261022 mirror1, Warlord's s2→s5 fission occurred at season80 with 12 pre-stores; no further Warlord fissions occurred through season128 while Rusher fissioned at seasons107 and121. The Warlord settlements generally had food above need by season128, although s1 still logged five shortfall seasons. Rusher launched 15 successful raids against s1 in seasons81–126 with reported loot totaling 268. The expansion gap was already present by season64, before these raids began, so the trace does not support blaming them for the delayed Warlord fission; whether the loot worsened its later population trajectory remains unisolated. Together the two mirrors reinforce that A3 has at least two failure paths: pre-existing demographic weakness with raids overlapping a collapse, and earlier fission/settlement lag. The trace does not identify a single safe rule adjustment.

The seed20261022 mirror1 expansion-loss match showed a different picture. Warlord s1 moved from 8 adults / 8.38 weighted capacity / 5.84 assigned food work at season81 to 5 / 5.00 / 2.59 at season128; s2 moved from 3 / 3.00 / 3.65 to 5 / 4.50 / 3.06. The new s5, founded from s2 at season80 with 12 stores, moved from 2 adults / 3.00 capacity / 2.48 assigned food work at season81 to 6 / 6.12 / 3.74 at season128. No Warlord settlement had a ledger shortfall at season128, although s1 had five shortfall rows in the window; its stores and pre-eat food recovered by the end. Rusher logged 15 successful raids against s1 during seasons81–126 (reported loot 268). Warlord recorded no further fission after the season80 s2→s5 founding through season128, while Rusher added settlements; this aligns with the expansion gap but does not expose Warlord's pending-fission eligibility or establish why it did not split again. The new work trace reports assigned work and realized forage/meat/fish/grain/fruit yield, not a causal per-job projection; sustainable income/capacity are separate estimates, and fission eligibility is not directly instrumented. Keep findings observational; no gameplay behavior or gates changed. Output: `/private/tmp/civsim-a3-collapse-food-work-current-seed20261021-22-64m.txt`.
