## B31 Legend vs Warlord: the 4-era research horizon costs Legend about 7 points (2026-11-02)

The `check-m10` B31 draw of 31% (5/16) was a low sample. On two 80-match blocks (first seeds 20261019 and 20261100, 64 minutes, `ai --duel legend warlord --seeds 40`), Legend wins 41% and 45% with the Warlord raid-withdrawal policy on and 41% and 49% with it off, so the policy is not the cause and the true rate is about 43-45% against >= 60%. Legend's mean Firsts Legacy is 698 against Warlord's 877 even though Legend reaches KEY-1 slightly earlier (6.6 vs 6.8 min) and runs 1.86x the Spark rollouts, so its extra search is not winning races.

Variants (same two blocks, pooled over 160 matches; each changed only the named Legend setting, pack or scratch-worktree override, nothing committed):

| Legend variant | Win % (block 1 / block 2) | Pooled |
|---|---|---|
| Production (horizon 4 eras, beam 16) | 41 / 45 | 43% |
| Beam 8 (horizon 4) | 42 / 46 | 44% |
| Horizon 3 eras (beam 16), both Spark and Strategist | 51 / 49 | 50% |
| Spark horizon 3, Strategist horizon 4 | 51 / 48 | 50% |
| Spark horizon 4, Strategist horizon 3 | 39 / 48 | 44% |
| Spark horizon 4, rival-frontier horizon 3 | 45 / 51 | 48% (inconclusive) |

Reading: beam width does nothing measurable; the Strategist horizon does nothing; the Spark's 4-era (96-season) research-forecast horizon is what pulls Legend below Warlord. Capping it at Warlord's 3 eras brings Legend to parity (about 50%), which is the mirror-match expectation, so Legend currently has no demonstrated edge over Warlord and 60% would need a real Legend capability (doc 14 §9.2 lists "coalition coordination timing", which is not implemented; `OpponentModel.FullCoalition` is treated like `Full`). The same pattern fits A4: Chieftain (2 eras, beam 4) wins 50% against Warlord, so deeper lookahead is not helping anywhere. No production change was made; thresholds are unchanged. The rival-frontier split was not decisive (SE about 4 points).

**B31 Legend horizon cap promoted (2026-11-02):** `content/registries/difficulty.yaml` now gives Legend `horizon_eras: 3` (beam 16 unchanged; doc 14 §9.2 notes it). Control and cap on the same blocks, 80 matches each, `ai --duel legend warlord --seeds 40`:

| First seed | 4 eras | 3 eras |
|---|---|---|
| 20261019 (tuned on) | 41% | 51% |
| 20261100 (tuned on) | 45% | 49% |
| 20261300 (fresh) | 46% | 44% |
| 20261400 (fresh) | 44% | 50% |
| All 320 matches | 44% | 48.5% |

The tuned-on blocks overstated the effect: on the two fresh blocks the gain is about +2 points (one standard error is roughly 3-4 points), so the cap is a small, harmless change that matches Warlord's horizon, and not a fix. **B31 remains open at about 48% against >= 60%.** Legend has no demonstrated edge over Warlord; closing the gate needs a real Legend capability (coalition coordination timing, doc 14 §9.2, not implemented) or a decision on the target, which I have not touched. Checks: 913/913 tests, toy/research/politics/bronze goldens unchanged, `validate --strict` clean, `content-pack.txt` golden refreshed (hash `8132d2e2…` to `679977fb…`) because the registry changed.

## B31: Legend coalition timing implemented, target relaxed to 45% (2026-11-02)

**Coalition timing (Legend only, `OpponentModel.FullCoalition`).** `StrategistTuning.CommittedElsewhereWindow` = +0.20 is added to a war's window (`ReadWarOptions`) and to the coalition plan's value (`Assess`) while the target's, or the forecast leader's, fighters are seen away on raids or assaults (`RivalBelief.PartiesAwaySeen`, public sightings only; this signal was computed but unused by the Strategist). Warlord and below are unchanged. The duel is 2v2 (seats 0 and 3 against 1 and 2) and teammates do not share beliefs, so teammate coordination is not implemented.

Four 80-match Legend-vs-Warlord blocks, the 3-era cap alone against the cap plus timing:

| First seed | Cap only | Cap + timing |
|---|---|---|
| 20261019 | 51% | 51% |
| 20261100 | 49% | 49% |
| 20261300 | 44% | 46% |
| 20261400 | 50% | 51% |
| Pooled 320 matches | 48.5% | 49.25% |

The first two blocks are identical in win rate and within a few points of Legacy, so the rule rarely changes a decision here; the pooled difference (+0.75 points) is far inside noise (one standard error is about 2.8 points). Legend remains at parity with Warlord. It is kept because it is the documented Legend capability (14 §7.3), uses only public information, is Legend-only and regresses nothing, not because it has been shown to help. A decision trace of how often it fires has not been done.

**B31 target relaxed from >= 60% to >= 45%** (user decision, 2026-11-02; value chosen by me): Legend must not be measurably worse than Warlord. Parity measures about 49%; the 4-era Legend sat at 43-44% over 320 matches, so 45% separates the two without requiring an edge the E3 content has not shown. `ai --check-m10` uses 16 matches here, which carries about +/-12 points of noise, so a miss on that draw should be confirmed on 80 matches before acting on it. The Legend-over-Warlord edge moves to the E4 design, as the 65% already did. Updated in `AiCommand.cs` (the gate), `17` §4, `22` §16 and `14` §10.3/§9.2.

Checks: 913/913 tests, toy/research/politics/bronze goldens unchanged, `validate --strict` clean. No golden refresh: these goldens do not include Legend seats.

**M10 AI check on pushed `main` `8ae42b6` (2026-11-02):** `ai --check-m10 --seeds 8 --first-seed 20261019 --minutes 64`, pack rebuilt from `content/`. Exit 1. Identical to the `2b07f2a` run (A1 88%, A2 69/94/88%, A3 100%/81%, A4 50%, A5 Builder 29.2 / Grower 22.9 / Wanderer 28.1 / Warlord 18.8 / Sage 26.0, A7 idle labor 1 season, B27 fallback 94% and dormant bronze 0.188, B29 26/26 within 15% but 6 horizon-censored, B30 pass) except for the Legend-affected lines: **B31 38% (6/16) against the new >= 45%: FAIL**, and B28 2/3 = 67% (n=3, was 1/1).

B31's miss is a low draw, not a regression: the same first seed over 40 seeds (80 matches) gives Legend 51%, and four 80-match blocks pool to 49%, while this official cohort is only the first 8 of those seeds (16 matches, about +/-12 points of noise). Under the stated rule (confirm a 16-match miss on 80 matches) B31 passes, but the official 16-match check will keep flipping around the threshold; moving B31 to a 32-seed sample (as B7/B8 use) would settle it and needs sign-off. The threshold stays at >= 45% and the first 8 seeds are unchanged. The B28 telemetry reads the same duel, so its case count grows with it (it was n=3). Cost: roughly 8-10 more minutes in `check-m10`. The 40-seed block at `20261019` (51%) contains these 32 seeds, so B31 is expected to pass.

## A7 idle labor: a measurement artifact, fixed in the detector (2026-11-03)

The single idle-labor season that `check-m10` kept reporting (seed 20261023, season 224, settlement s24, figure 905, age 14, job `Rest`, food security 0.156; earlier runs showed it at other seeds, e.g. 20261025 s10) is not idle labor. An instrumented rerun of the seed (temporary debug print in the job-assignment loop, reverted) showed that during the season's work assignment figure 905 had `Age = 13`: the adult-only civic branch skipped them, the settlement's food pool (0.09 work) was used up by the one other adult, and a child gets only leftover capacity. The figure turned 14 at the season boundary (`AgePopulationByOneYear` runs once a year), and the harness sampled adults after the advance, so it counted a child who had just had a birthday as an idle adult in a settlement with only two adults (1/2 = 50% over the 15% threshold).

**Fix (harness only):** `Tournament` now snapshots which figures were under `M10TelemetryRules.AdultWorkAge` (14) before each advance and excludes them from the adult and idle counts, in the same way it already excludes levy and siege hosts. The thresholds, the economy and every golden are unchanged. The exact 8-seed, 64-minute A7 check (`ai --check-a7 --seeds 8 --first-seed 20261019 --minutes 64`) now reports idle labor 0 seasons and every other detector 0 (A7 CHECK PASSED); before it was 1 season. The seed-20261023 case alone also read 0.5 per match before the change (2 matches).

## B27 dormant bronze: the detector judged the AI by ground truth, fixed to use its own map (2026-11-03)

`check-m10` and `check-b27` reported dormant bronze 3/16 = 0.188 per match (target < 0.1). The three flagged seats (seeds 20261019 civ 1, 20261020 civ 2, 20261025 civ 0) were all in the same state: MET-04 Dormant (done, no arsenical access), MET-05 Available, no metal access, a "recovery option" per the detector, and no Compact or Mine order for more than 8 seasons. An instrumented rerun (new `B27_DORMANT` line with `arsSite`, `arsOreSource`, `arsSiteKnown`, plus per-season traces for any seat in a dormant streak) showed why the AI never mined: **the arsenical vein the detector called an option was not identified on the AI's own map** (`arsSiteKnown=None` for all three). `BeliefBuilder.UpdateKnownDeposits` identifies deep ore veins only inside the civilization's own territory with MIN-04 and nearby outcrop evidence, and these sites lay outside it. The detector used `NearestMineSite`, which reads the world's ground truth, so it flagged a mine the AI could not see. That breaks the fog rule the AI plays by (15 D10), the same kind of artifact as the A7 idle-labor case.

**Fix (measurement only):** `PoliticsSystem.NearestMineSite` gained an overload taking an `identified` filter (the existing call keeps its behavior); `Tournament` now passes a filter that accepts a tile only if the AI seat's belief map shows that deposit kind there, for both the tin and arsenic options. The tin-seller option was already knowledge-based. Thresholds, the AI and every golden are unchanged. A unit test pins the filter. Exact 8-seed, 64-minute `check-b27` (seeds from 20261019): **dormant-route blunders 0/16 = 0.000, tin/arsenical recovery 30/32 = 94% (target >= 80%): PASS** (was 3/16 = 0.188, FAIL). The two unrecovered seats (20261022 civ 1, 20261023 civ 3) are unchanged and fall within the 80% allowance.

**Not fixed, noted for later:** in all three seats the Strategist spent its mandate on a Levy against the same Tin Folk village every ~6 seasons (EV 80-157) for 25+ seasons without ever getting tin; the Levy is not counted as a recovery action because a Levy is an assault, not a mine or a compact. That is a real planning weakness (repeating a failing conquest) but B27 does not measure it, and changing the Levy valuation would move the war balance. A cooldown after a failed Tin Folk Levy is the obvious candidate if it matters in play.

|---|---|---|---|

| A2 Grower / Builder / Wanderer | 69% (11/16) / 94% / 88% | >= 70% | **Grower fails** |
| A3 | 100% survive, 81% win | >= 80% / >= 70% | pass |
| A4 | 38% | 25-40% | pass |
| A5 | Builder 29.2, Grower 22.9, Wanderer 28.1, Warlord 18.8, Sage 26.0 | each <= 30% | pass |
| A7 | all detectors 0 including idle labor | 0 | **pass** (was 1 season) |
| B27 | fallback 94% (30/32); dormant bronze 0/16 | >= 80% / < 0.1 | **pass** (was 0.188) |

## A2 now judged on 32 seeds; Grower still misses its cohort by one match (2026-11-03, user sign-off)

`check-m10` and `check-m6` now run the three Warlord-vs-opening duels (A2) on `max(seeds, 32)` seeds (64 matches per script, about +/-6 points of noise instead of +/-12) when the check is acceptance quality; diagnostic runs keep the requested seeds. The threshold stays at >= 70% and the first 8 seeds are unchanged (constant `M10AiAcceptanceStatus.MinimumWideSampleSeeds`, shared with B31). Cost: about 144 more matches in `check-m10`.

Result on the exact 32-seed cohort (first seed 20261019, 64 minutes, pack from `471d98f`): **Builder 88% (pass), Wanderer 83% (pass), Grower 69% = 44/64 (fail by one match: a pass needs 45/64 = 70.3%)**. The wider sample did not flip Grower: its first 16 matches were 11/16 = 68.75% and all 64 are 44/64 = 68.75%.

An independent block with different seeds (`--duel warlord grower --seeds 40 --first-seed 20261100`, 80 matches) gives **75%**. Pooled with the 64-match cohort that is 104/144 = 72.2% (one standard error about 3.7 points), so Grower's true rate looks to be around 72%, above the bar but not by much; a 64-match draw at 72% falls under 70% roughly four times in ten. Reading: the miss is cohort luck, not a defect, and I did not change the threshold or pick a friendlier cohort (a cohort chosen after seeing results would not be a test). The official A2 Grower line will therefore keep reading FAIL on this exact cohort until Warlord-vs-Grower improves by a match or two or the cohort is widened further. Candidates if you want it to pass honestly: a real Warlord-vs-Grower gain (the earlier fission and posture diagnostics in 22 §16 found no single cause), or a still wider sample such as 64 seeds (about +/-4 points), which would cost roughly twice as much again.

**A2 Grower accepted as passed (2026-11-03, project owner's decision):** the exact 32-seed cohort still prints `A2 grower: 69% ... FAIL` (44/64, one match under the 70% line), and `check-m10` will keep printing it and its "measurable gates FAIL" summary on account of that line alone. The owner reviewed the evidence (independent 80-match block 75%, pooled 104/144 = 72.2%, no identified defect) and called A2 passed. The harness, threshold and cohort are unchanged, so the line is a known, accepted near-miss rather than a regression; any other FAIL in a future `check-m10` is new and should be investigated. Doc 17's A2 row records the acceptance.

**Accepted-exception mechanism (2026-11-03):** at the owner's request the A2 acceptance is explicit in the harness instead of a doc-only note. `M10AiAcceptanceStatus.IsAcceptedException` accepts exactly one gate, `A2 grower`, and only while Warlord still wins at least `AcceptedA2GrowerFloor` = 65% of the Grower duel (the floor is my choice: below the observed 68.75%-to-75% band, so a real regression fails again). `CheckM6` then prints `A2 grower: 69% over 64 matches FAIL (ACCEPTED by the project owner, docs/MVP/07d)` and does not count it against the core check, so `check-m10` can end with "M10 AI CORE CHECK PASSED" and "measurable gates PASS" when everything else passes. Diagnostic runs are unaffected, the 70% threshold and the cohort are unchanged, and a unit test pins the rule (Grower at or above 65% accepted; below it, Builder, Wanderer and every other gate not). This supersedes the earlier remark that the summary would keep reading FAIL on account of that line.

## B29 KEY-4 forecast fidelity: passes once the observation window is long enough (2026-11-03)

B29 showed `26/26 forecasts within 15%, median 1.7%, 6 KEY-4 horizon-censored` and so read INCOMPLETE in every `check-m10`: the integrated check stops observing at the 64-minute capture horizon, and six of the 32 AI seats finish KEY-4 later than that, so their forecasts could not be scored (a censored forecast cannot pass by design, 22 §16). The harness already had the remedy (`--observation-minutes` continues the same simulations after capture). Standalone `ai --check-b29 --seeds 8 --first-seed 20261019 --minutes 64 --observation-minutes 100`, pack from `main` `5dc20ea`: **32/32 forecasts within 15%, median 2.0% (target <= 6%), 32 of 32 seats sampled, 0 missing at capture, 0 censored: B29 PASS.** Observing to minute 100 completed every KEY-4, so the forecast itself needed no change; the 6 seats that were censored scored within tolerance and moved the median from 1.7% to 2.0%.

**Change (harness only):** `check-m10` now passes `M10AiAcceptanceStatus.ResolveB29ObservationMinutes(seeds, minutes)` to B29, which is `max(minutes, 100)` for acceptance-quality samples (8 seeds and 64 minutes or more) and the capture horizon for diagnostic ones; a unit test pins it. Standalone `--check-b29` keeps its default of observing only to the capture horizon unless `--observation-minutes` is given. Cost: B29's simulations in `check-m10` now run 100 game-minutes instead of 64, roughly 25-30 more minutes of the check. This follows the 22 §16 text ("extend the observation window before acceptance"); it changes how long B29 is observed, not its 15% / 6% thresholds.

**M10 AI check on pushed `main` `090de8e` (2026-11-03): every measured gate passes.** `ai --check-m10 --seeds 8 --first-seed 20261019 --minutes 64`, pack rebuilt from `content/` (hash `6436cd73...`). The run prints `M10 AI CORE CHECK PASSED` and `M10 measurable gates PASS; full B26-B30 result remains INCOMPLETE.`

| Gate | Result | Target | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | 88% (16 matches) | >= 85% | pass |
| A2 Grower / Builder / Wanderer | 69% / 88% / 83% (64 matches each) | >= 70% | Builder and Wanderer pass; Grower `FAIL (ACCEPTED by the project owner)` |
| A3 | 100% survive, 81% win | >= 80% / >= 70% | pass |
| A4 | 38% | 25-40% | pass |
| A5 | Builder 29.2, Grower 22.9, Wanderer 28.1, Warlord 18.8, Sage 26.0 | each <= 30% | pass |
| A7 | every detector 0 | 0 | pass |
| B27 | fallback 94% (30/32); dormant bronze 0/16 | >= 80% / < 0.1 | pass |
| B28 | 8/10 = 80% | >= 80% | pass (on the line) |

| B30 | pass | | pass |
| B31 | 50% over 64 matches | >= 45% | pass |

Reading: the A1-A5/A7 and B27-B31 gates the check measures are all green; the only non-PASS label is the owner-accepted A2 Grower near-miss. The process still exits 1 and prints "full B26-B30 result remains INCOMPLETE" by design: `CheckM10` always returns 1 because A6 (exploit search, `ai --exploit`) and A8 (fog fairness) are separate acceptance gates, so the combined B26 gate stays open until those two are recorded as passed alongside this run. B28 sits exactly on its 80% line and is worth a re-check on a different first seed. Not covered by this check: A6, A8, B6, B12 70-minute coverage, B17 KEY-4 completion, B18 and B19.

## A6 exploit search and A8 fog fairness on `main` `576ccca`: both pass, B26 closes (2026-11-03)

**A6:** `ai --pack civsim.pack --exploit --generations 417 --children 24 --seeds 1 --screen-minutes 1 --minutes 64` (the exact-budget command of 22 §16). 10,009 distinct candidate policies were screened at a 1-minute horizon (screen seed 20266019), 16 diverse finalists were validated at the 64-minute chapter horizon on 3 fresh mirrored seed pairs each (validation seeds 20270019-20270021). **No finalist exceeded 55% against Warlord: the best won 33%, eight won 17%, four 0%, and the rest 17-33%. EXPLOIT SEARCH PASS** (exit 0; it applies to this searched candidate set, not all possible policies). The finalists did not collapse onto one trick: they mixed AGR/ART/BEL/COM research orders, raid and fortify seasons from never to season 89, and party sizes 4-14. The run took well under an hour. The pack was rebuilt from `content/` (hash `6436cd73...`); `576ccca` differs from the A1-A5 run's `090de8e` by documentation only.

**A8:** `dotnet test CivSim.slnx -c Release --filter FullyQualifiedName~FogFairness` passes 2/2 (25 s), including the unseen-Bronze case, so seats see no hidden state (docs 15 D10, 22 §7 evidence rules).

**B26 status:** with the full `check-m10` of `090de8e` (A1-A5, A7, B27-B31 all passing, A2 Grower owner-accepted) and these two, every A-gate of 14 §10.3 (A1-A8) has been re-measured under the E3 Legacy units and passes on current `main`. `check-m10` still exits 1 by design because it cannot see A6 and A8; the combined result lives in this record. Open items outside the AI gates: B6 Year-64 endings (11/32 earlier), B12 70-minute coverage, B17 KEY-4 completion, B18 and B19.

Measured for the M10 status row: `politics --check-m10 --seeds 24 --minutes 80`, `research --check-m10` on seeds 20261019-20261050 (B6) and on 20261019 and 777 (B5), `ai --check-b7 --seeds 32 --minutes 64` and `ai --check-b8 --seeds 32 --minutes 64`. Pack rebuilt from `content/` (hash `6436cd73...`).

| Gate | Result | Target | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| B5 | KEY-4 Council median 61.7, E3 median 21.0, all 8 civs in band | 57-64 / 19.75 +-25% | pass |
| B6 | chapter-end median 63.5, replay identical, **Year-64 endings 11/32 = 34%** | median reported; <= 10% at Year 64 | **fail** (ending share) |
| B7 | 70.3% | >= 65% | pass |
| B8 | 25/32 pairs (78.1%), mean +76.3 | >= 60% | pass |
| B17 | 10.6 settlements/civ, claimed land 41.1%, borders 91/93, Town 93.8%, City 32.3%; **KEY-4 completed 93/96** | all in band, completion 96/96 | **fail** on completion only |
| B18 | **Dominion 55.5%**, Mastery 30.4%, Firsts 14.1%, Wonders 0% | Dominion 45-55% | **fail** by 0.5 points |
| B19 | cohesion 62.7, **secessions 0.01 per civ per E3**, cascades 0/24, exposures 93/94 | 0.03-0.08, 100% complete | **fail** |
| B20 | 10.7 raids, war 75.0%, capture 20.8% | 3-12, >= 50%, 20-50% | pass |

Control for B17-B20 with Chieftain's Council prior at 5% (the pre-A4 value, same tree): B17 94/96 completion and borders 86/94, B18 Dominion 55.3%, B19 **secessions 0.06 (in band)** with exposures 94/96, B20 pass. So the A4 change (prior 20%) moved this Chieftain-4-AI cohort: secessions from 0.06 to 0.01 (about 6 events to 1, so partly noise, but a lower count is plausible with a weaker Chieftain) and one more civ missing KEY-4. B17 and B19 fail on every tree because their rule demands that all 96 civs complete KEY-4 within the 80-minute ceiling; the 11-01 trace shows the misses are fragile founder bands (demographic variance, no research-pace lever), and the held-out cohort completed 96/96, so which civs miss changes with any unrelated rule. Everything else in B17 is in band.

Reading: the remaining failures are four marginal or statistical ones. B6's 34% Year-64 share means a third of matches have no rival KEY-4 by about minute 61 (first KEY-4 lands at season 249-251 in the capped ones), so KEY-4 is slightly slower than the chapter cap assumes; the median end (63.5) is inside the design, only the share misses. Fixing it by pace means moving E3 EraBase, which reshuffles every golden and AI gate just closed; relaxing the share target or accepting it is the cheap alternative.

## B6, B17, B18, B19 thresholds relaxed and re-measured (2026-11-03, owner sign-off)

At the owner's approval ("Yes, relax them") four thresholds now match the measured state, with the evidence above (non-AI snapshot on `ffbb63d`, the 72-seed politics run and the 11-01 founder-band trace):

| Gate | Was | Now | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| B6 Year-64 endings | <= 10% | **<= 40%** | 11/32 = 34%; the chapter-end median (63.5) and replay identity pass; an earlier KEY-4 means re-tuning E3 pace, every golden and the AI gates just closed |
| B17 KEY-4 completion | 100% of civs | **>= 95%** | 279/288 = 96.9% on 72 seeds; the misses are fragile founder bands (demographic variance), and which civs miss changes with any unrelated rule |
| B19 exposure completion | 100% of civs | **>= 95%** (same helper, `PoliticsM10.IsKey4CompletionInRange`) | same cause |
| B18 Dominion cap | <= 55% | **<= 57%** (`PoliticsM10.B18DominionMaxPercent`) | steady 55.2-55.9% over every run since the B12 promotion; Mastery and Firsts are in band |

Code: `ResearchM10.IsB6YearCapRateInRange` (<= 40%) and the two `PoliticsM10` helpers/constants above, with unit tests (937 pass). Docs: 22 §16 B6/B17/B18/B19 rows and the T24 row, 17 §4.4 B6 row.

**Re-measured on `main` (this tree, pack hash `6436cd73...`):** B6 on the 32-seed research sample: median end 63.5, Year-64 endings 11/32 (target <= 40%), replay identical: **PASS**. `politics --check-m10 --seeds 72 --minutes 80`: B17 **PASS** (10.6 settlements/civ, claimed land 42.0%, borders 257/279, Town 91.0%, City 25.3%, KEY-4 279/288), B18 **PASS** (Dominion 55.9%, Mastery 30.1%, Firsts 14.0%), B19 **PASS** (cohesion 62.4, secessions 0.04, cascades 0/72, exposures 279/284), B20 **PASS**.

**B17-B20 sample size (disclosed change):** on the 24-seed draw the same run gives B17 PASS, B18 PASS, B20 PASS and **B19 FAIL on secessions 0.01** (1 event against a 0.03-0.08 band): a 24-seed draw has only about six expected secessions, so 0-1 events is ordinary noise, and the 72-seed rate (0.04) is in band. I therefore wrote 72 seeds into the 22 §16 B17-B20 rows (the harness is unchanged and already accepts any sample of 24 or more). If you would rather keep 24 seeds as the official cohort, B19 would read FAIL on this exact draw until a change or a different seed range moves it; say so and I will revert that wording.

**Where M10 stands:** the AI, chapter-end, territory, legacy and cohesion gates pass. Open: B12 70-minute coverage (52-70% on four source-backed pairs against 70%) and the human gates B1-B3. B4, B9-B11, B13-B16, B21, B23 and B24 were not re-run today and should be re-confirmed once together.

## B12 metal chain on `main` `cc31af6`: the stated T18 target is met, narrowly (2026-11-03)

The "B12 70-minute coverage" figure that stayed open (70% on the training pairs, 52% on held-out pairs, 07d 2026-11-01) was the **source-backed** split, a stricter internal diagnostic. The gate itself (22 §16 B12, T18) is "at least 70% of tool-using workers on rung 6 within 12 seasons of MET-04/05 **where access holds**", which is the report's **live-input-access cohort** (continuous input access through +12) and prints as `target >= 70% where access holds`. Measured with `research --report-metal --minutes 70`, 8 seeds per set (four pairs), all on the current pack:

| Seed set | Live-input-access cohort (the gate) | Rows at >= 70% | Source-backed (stricter diagnostic) | Stock-only |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| training pairs 20261019/777, 20261020/778, 31337/9001, 424242/2024 | **71%** (1904/2673), per-settlement median 85% | 142 of 223 | 59% (702/1199) | 82% |
| fresh 20261021/779 ... 20261024/782 | **73%** (1962/2687), median 88% | 131 of 209 | 67% (653/969) | 76% |
| fresh 20261025/783 ... 20261028/786 | **67%** (2203/3265), median 80% | 147 of 255 | 56% (831/1473) | 77% |
| pooled, 24 seeds | **70.4%** (6069/8625) | | 60.0% (2186/3641) | |

The fixture half passes too (`research --metal-fixture`): rung 6 reaches 70% at season 4-5 and 86-97% by season 12 for 40-100 people; after tin is cut the fallback reason is `NoTin` the next season and the tin-bronze share declines gradually (T10 73 of 77 to 68 at +12 with arsenic in range, 67 without), with no cliff; with no charcoal 10 L of copper ore smelts 0.00 batches, and with 2 L of charcoal it smelts 1.25; WOD-04 holds the 100-person forest drawdown at 3% (71% without it).

Reading: B12 meets its stated target on the pooled sample but with essentially no margin (70.4% against 70%, and one of three 8-seed sets is below at 67%); the promotion of the four B12 rules (07d 2026-10-31) is what moved it, and the earlier sessions' further levers (chain-cap multipliers, housing release, unobtainable-ore block, MineHead wood) were rejected. The stricter source-backed split stays at 56-67%: settlements that must open their own camp and build their first Forge or Kiln inside the 12-season window are the ones that miss, a small-village timing effect the earlier diagnosis traced to a 10% build cap, not to a missing rule. I did not tune further. If the margin matters, the next lever is the ones in the 2026-10-31 diagnosis (build allowance for the first Forge or Kiln in a small village), at the cost of re-checking every economy-dependent gate.

## M10 closed: human gates B1-B3 reported passing (2026-11-04)

The project owner reported on 2026-11-04 that B1 (agency), B2 (bronze is legible) and B3 (want more after the Bronze end screen, and can explain an AI action) were playtested with real players and are all passing. This record rests on that report: the participant count, the answers and the per-person records are not in the repo (the M9 protocol asks for anonymous records with the denominator preserved; if they exist, add them next to `m9-playtest-protocol.md` so the E4 design pass can use the findings). With the automated gates measured on 2026-11-03 (every AI gate A1-A8 and B26-B31, B5-B8, B10, B12, B14, B15, B17-B25), M10 is complete.

What the record does and does not cover: the automated gate results were measured on `main` through `577d431`/`cc31af6` and then `bf7174c` for docs; four commits from other sessions landed after the AI gates were run, one of them (`4375958`, block peace during unresolved assaults) a sim change, so no full `check-m10` has been run on the current tip. Known accepted near-misses: A2 Grower (owner-accepted, pooled 72.2%), B12 (70.4% pooled, thin), and the thresholds relaxed on 2026-11-03 (B6, B17, B18, B19). Known weakness left alone: the Strategist repeats a failing Tin Folk Levy for 25+ seasons (B27 does not measure it).
